and my hands still keep opening containers like I am not the one in charge.\n- key: S051\n  name: Rapid_Eating\n  description: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n  manifestation: I eat so fast during those times that I barely taste anything, just standing at the dining room table with the microwave and boxes around me, swallowing one bite while I am already reaching for the next.\n- key: S052\n  name: Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full\n  description: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n  manifestation: I keep going until my stomach is tight and painful, and then I end up sitting very still on the couch because even walking upstairs feels uncomfortable.\n- key: S053\n  name: Eating_Without_Hunger\n  description: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n  manifestation: A lot of the time I am not physically hungry at all, because I may have had a normal dinner already, but later I still go looking through the coolers or pantry shelves like food is something I have to finish.\n- key: S054\n  name: Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment\n  description: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n  manifestation: I make a point of doing it when my husband is asleep or when the contractors are gone, because I would be mortified for anyone to see the amount I can put away after acting so controlled all day at work.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: The binge eating occurs, on average, at least once a week for 3 months\n  manifestation: When I look back eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. That sounds so... heavy, I guess? Like I've committed a crime or something. \n\nI mean, like I said, I feel a little embarrassed, maybe a bit disappointed in myself for not having better willpower in that moment. But I really try to just brush it off and focus on the positive. I think if I sat around feeling guilty, it would just make things worse, right? I try to just tell myself, \"Okay, Taylor, you slipped up, but tomorrow is a fresh start.\" \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I feel a deep sense of guilt or anything like that. Just... a little annoyed with myself. I hope that's an okay answer? I feel like that would just make it harder to be productive the next day, right? \n\nSo... no, I wouldn't say I criticize myself. Just a quick thought of, oh, I probably shouldn't have done that, and then I try to let it go. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I don't take it seriously! I just think being too hard on myself wouldn't be very helpful. a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n  manifestation: Once I start, there is this awful switch that flips where I can be thinking, Taylor, put it away, and my hands still keep opening containers like I am not the one in charge.\n- key: S051\n  name: Rapid_Eating\n  description: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n  manifestation: I eat so fast during those times that I barely taste anything, just standing at the dining room table with the microwave and boxes around me, swallowing one bite while I am already reaching for the next.\n- key: S052\n  name: Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full\n  description: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n  manifestation: I keep going until my stomach is tight and painful, and then I end up sitting very still on the couch because even walking upstairs feels uncomfortable.\n- key: S053\n  name: Eating_Without_Hunger\n  description: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n  manifestation: A lot of the time I am not physically hungry at all, because I may have had a normal dinner already, but later I still go looking through the coolers or pantry shelves like food is something I have to finish.\n- key: S054\n  name: Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment\n  description: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n  manifestation: I make a point of doing it when my husband is asleep or when the contractors are gone, because I would be mortified for anyone to see the amount I can put away after acting so controlled all day at work.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: The binge eating occurs, on average, at least once a week for 3 months\n  manifestation: When I look back through my journal, there has barely been a week in the last several months without at least one of these nights, and most weeks it happens several times.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I have stopped inviting people over and have skipped some evening routines like yoga or gardening chores because I feel too ashamed and preoccupied after these episodes.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since the kitchen remodel started about ten months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 54 / first_line_supervisor_of_office_or_administrative_support_worker / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S049\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like I completely lose my mind or anything, but... I guess sometimes it can feel a little bit hard to just stop? \n\nIt's sort of like... once I get going, it's almost like a switch flips? I mean, I'm still aware, obviously. I'll be sitting there thinking, \"Okay, Taylor, that's enough, you can put this away now,\" but... it's just a little tricky because my hands just kind of keep opening the next container, almost like I'm not totally the one steering the ship right in that exact moment. \n\nBut I mean, I'm sure everyone gets a little carried away sometimes when something tastes good, right? It's just a momentary thing. I really hope that doesn't make me sound completely out of control or anything... does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how often? Well, I try not to focus too much on the frequency, because I really want to stay positive about it all. But... Um... distress? Well, I wouldn't use a strong word like that, necessarily. I mean, I try to keep a very positive outlook on life, and I know there are people dealing with much worse things, so I really try not to complain!

It is almost like I am not the one in charge right then. I know I should just be able to stop, and I feel so silly even admitting it out loud. I don't really think so. It often just happens when I'm sitting at home, feeling perfectly fine, which makes it feel even more foolish, honestly. Is it... is it strange if I don't have a clear reason like being upset? Um... control? Well, I like to think of myself as a very in-control person, especially at work. You know, managing the office, you have to be on top of everything! And I really try to bring that same discipline home with me.

I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, I just haven't really noticed a connection like that.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after you've eaten a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... I mostly just feel a little uncomfortable? Physically, I mean, just from being a bit too full. And, well, maybe a little embarrassed with myself. But I really try not to dwell on it. \n\nI usually just tidy up the kitchen right away, you know, get rid of the containers and wrappers so everything looks nice and neat again, and I just try to put it out of my mind. I always tell myself tomorrow will be a better day. I mean, nobody likes feeling like they overdid it at dinner, right? I'm sure that's a pretty normal way to react. \n\nI hope I'm answering your question the right way... I just don't have a very, um, dramatic reaction to it, if that makes sense? I just feel a bit silly and try to move on. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty? I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. That sounds so... heavy, I guess? Like I've committed a crime or something. \n\nI mean, like I said, I feel a little embarrassed, maybe a bit disappointed in myself for not having better willpower in that moment. But I really try to just brush it off and focus on the positive. I think if I sat around feeling guilty, it would just make things worse, right? I try to just tell myself, \"Okay, Taylor, you slipped up, but tomorrow is a fresh start.\" \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I feel a deep sense of guilt or anything like that. Just... a little annoyed with myself. I hope that's an okay answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you an accurate picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself after eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... criticize? Not really. I mean, I try to be pretty gentle with myself. Like I mentioned, I just tidy up and try to move forward. I don't sit there calling myself names or beating myself up about it. I feel like that would just make it harder to be productive the next day, right? \n\nSo... no, I wouldn't say I criticize myself. Just a quick thought of, oh, I probably shouldn't have done that, and then I try to let it go. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I don't take it seriously! I just think being too hard on myself wouldn't be very helpful. Is that... does that answer what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like I completely lose my mind or anything, but... I guess sometimes it can feel a little bit hard to just stop? \n\nIt's sort of like... once I get going, it's almost like a switch flips? I mean, I'm still aware, obviously. I'll be sitting there thinking, \"Okay, Taylor, that's enough, you can put this away now,\" but... it's just a little tricky because my hands just kind of keep opening the next container, almost like I'm not totally the one steering the ship right in that exact moment. \n\nBut I mean, I'm sure everyone gets a little carried away sometimes when something tastes good, right? It's just a momentary thing. I really hope that doesn't make me sound completely out of control or anything... does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself happen?\n\nPatient: Um... how often? Well, I try not to focus too much on the frequency, because I really want to stay positive about it all. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, and I know you need me to be... it's somewhat regular. \n\nI actually keep a little journal, just to track my days and stay organized with work and everything. And... well, when I look back through it, there has barely been a week in the last several months without at least one of these nights. And, um, most weeks it happens several times. \n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot, and I really don't want you to think I'm just letting myself go completely. I'm working very hard on it! But I want to be a good patient and give you the exact information you need. Is that... is that the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself interfere with your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Um... interfere? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad friend or like I'm just being lazy! I just feel a bit embarrassed in those moments, I think. Um... guilty? Well, I try not to use words like that if I can help it. I mean, guilt is such a strong, heavy word, isn't it? I really try to focus on the positive and just tell myself I'll do better next time. I think it's important to be forgiving of ourselves, don't you?

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... yes, maybe a little bit. Sometimes after it happens, I just look at the wrappers and think, *Oh, Taylor, why did you do that?* It's just a bit of a letdown, I suppose. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I really do try to just sweep it up and move on. I certainly don't sit around wallowing in it! It's just a fleeting feeling of... well, wishing I had been a bit more sensible. 

I hope that's a normal way to feel about it? I really don't want to sound like I'm making excuses for myself. Does that make sense to you?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, yes, I suppose it's exactly like what I was trying to describe a little earlier. I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I know your time is valuable! 

But yes, it's... it's just like I mentioned before. Once I actually start eating, it's like that awful little switch flips in my brain. I can literally be sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, *Taylor, you really need to put this away now*, but... it's like my hands just aren't listening to me. They just keep opening another container, and then another. 

It's the strangest feeling, almost like I'm just watching myself do it and I'm not the one in charge anymore. I know that probably sounds terribly weak of me, and I really do try so hard to be disciplined in every other part of my life! But in those specific moments... yes, I suppose "losing control" is the right way to put it, even though I hate to admit it. Does that... does that clarify things a bit better for you? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.

It's just a momentary thing. I really hope that doesn't make me sound completely out of control or anything... does that make sense? Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after one of these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Well, I try not to use words like that if I can help it. I mean, guilt is such a strong, heavy word, isn't it? I really try to focus on the positive and just tell myself I'll do better next time. I think it's important to be forgiving of ourselves, don't you?\n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... yes, maybe a little bit. Sometimes after it happens, I just look at the wrappers and think, *Oh, Taylor, why did you do that?* It's just a bit of a letdown, I suppose. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I really do try to just sweep it up and move on. I certainly don't sit around wallowing in it! It's just a fleeting feeling of... well, wishing I had been a bit more sensible. \n\nI hope that's a normal way to feel about it? I really don't want to sound like I'm making excuses for myself. Does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes because you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose it's exactly like what I was trying to describe a little earlier. I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I know your time is valuable! \n\nBut yes, it's... it's just like I mentioned before. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... significant guilt? Well, I know we just touched on this a moment ago, and I certainly don't want to sound like a broken record for you! But... no, I really wouldn't say it's *significant*, and definitely not all the time. 

Like I was trying to explain earlier, I really do my best to just brush it off. I have a whole team at the office relying on me, so I just can't afford to let myself sink into, you know, deep, heavy guilt. I think if I let it become a significant thing, I wouldn't be able to function as well as I need to! So it really is just that brief moment of wishing I hadn't done it, and then I make myself pull it together and move on. 

I hope I'm answering that right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the exact picture you need, and I'm not entirely sure I'd ever use a strong word like "significant" for it. Is that okay?

I just don't want to make a big fuss over something that I should be able to handle myself. \n\nI really try to just brush it off and start fresh the next day. Is that... does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... control? Well, I like to think of myself as a very in-control person, especially at work. You know, managing the office, you have to be on top of everything! And I really try to bring that same discipline home with me. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being perfectly honest with you—and I know you need the whole truth to help me—it doesn't always feel like I'm the one steering the ship during those times. It's almost like... well, once I start, there's this awful little switch that flips. I can actually be sitting there thinking in my head, *Taylor, put it away, you don't need this*, but... my hands just keep opening containers anyway. It's like I'm not even the one in charge in that moment. \n\nI know that sounds so silly, a grown woman not being able to stop opening a package of food. I really hope you don't think I'm just weak-willed! I'm usually so responsible. It's just... in those specific moments, it feels a bit out of my hands. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel better about yourself if you could control your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... feel better about myself? Well, I try very hard to maintain a positive self-image, you know! I'm really quite proud of the work I do at the office and the life I've built. It would be so nice to not have that... that silly embarrassment hanging over my head when I'm at home. I just want to be the best, most responsible version of myself, I guess. I really hope that doesn't sound too cliché? I just think it would make things a lot smoother for me, and I wouldn't have to worry about it anymore. Is that... does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after one of these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... guilty? Well, I try not to use words like that if I can help it. I mean, guilt is such a strong, heavy word, isn't it? I really try to focus on the positive and just tell myself I'll do better next time. I think it's important to be forgiving of ourselves, don't you?\n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... yes, maybe a little bit. Sometimes after it happens, I just look at the wrappers and think, *Oh, Taylor, why did you do that?* It's just a bit of a letdown, I suppose. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I really do try to just sweep it up and move on. I certainly don't sit around wallowing in it! It's just a fleeting feeling of... well, wishing I had been a bit more sensible. \n\nI hope that's a normal way to feel about it? I really don't want to sound like I'm making excuses for myself. Does that... is that the kind of information you needed?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... distress? I guess if I'm being completely honest, and I know you need me to be... it's somewhat regular. \n\nI actually keep a little journal, just to track my days and stay organized with work and everything. And... well, when I look back through it, there has barely been a week in the last several months without at least one of these nights. And, um, most weeks it happens several times. \n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot, and I really don't want you to think I'm just letting myself go completely. I'm working very hard on it! Does that... is that the kind of information you needed?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... distress? Well, I wouldn't use a strong word like that, necessarily. I mean, I try to keep a very positive outlook on life, and I know there are people dealing with much worse things, so I really try not to complain! \n\nBut... It's more of a frustration than actual distress, I think. I just don't want to make a big fuss over something that I should be able to handle myself. \n\nI really try to just brush it off and start fresh the next day. I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I know your time is valuable! \n\nBut yes, it's... it's just like I mentioned before. Once I actually start eating, it's like that awful little switch flips in my brain. I can literally be sitting there, fully aware, telling myself, *Taylor, you really need to put this away now*, but... it's like my hands just aren't listening to me. They just keep opening another container, and then another. \n\nIt's the strangest feeling, almost like I'm just watching myself do it and I'm not the one in charge anymore. I know that probably sounds terribly weak of me, and I really do try so hard to be disciplined in every other part of my life! But in those specific moments... yes, I suppose \"losing control\" is the right way to put it, even though I hate to admit it. Does that... does that clarify things a bit better for you? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are too hard on yourself after one of these episodes of eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... too hard on myself? Well, I try not to be! I really believe in the power of positive thinking, you know? I think if you're always beating yourself up, you just get stuck in a rut, and I certainly don't have time for that with my job and everything else I have going on. I always try to look on the bright side and just remind myself that tomorrow is a new day. \n\nBut... I mean, I suppose I do get a little frustrated with myself sometimes. I just want to make sure I'm handling it the best way I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience significant guilt after eating a large amount of food by yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... significant guilt? Well, I know we just touched on this a moment ago, and I certainly don't want to sound like a broken record for you! But... no, I really wouldn't say it's *significant*, and definitely not all the time. \n\nLike I was trying to explain earlier, I really do my best to just brush it off. I have a whole team at the office relying on me, so I just can't afford to let myself sink into, you know, deep, heavy guilt. I think if I let it become a significant thing, I wouldn't be able to function as well as I need to! So it really is just that brief moment of wishing I hadn't done it, and then I make myself pull it together and move on. \n\nI hope I'm answering that right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the exact picture you need, and I'm not entirely sure I'd ever use a strong word like \"significant\" for it. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the guilt or embarrassment you feel after eating a large amount of food by yourself is something that you often think about or dwell on after the episode has passed?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I really try my best not to do that. I hope I'm not sounding too repetitive, but I just don't think it does any good to sit around and dwell on things once they're over and done with. \n\nI feel like if I let myself just sit there and ruminate on the embarrassment or the... the slip-up, it would really start to affect my mood, and then that would affect my work. And my team really needs me to be present and focused, you know? So I just make a conscious effort to put it completely out of my mind. I just tell myself, *well, that happened, let's move forward*, and I throw myself back into my routine or my tasks for the day. \n\nI really try to be resilient and not let it drag me down. I hope that's a healthy way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by trying to stay positive and not dwelling on the negative. I feel a little bad because I think I'm just repeating myself, and I really don't want to be difficult for you! But... no, I honestly wouldn't say it weighs heavily on me at all. \n\nI know we're kind of circling the same idea, and I just... I really do try to nip any of those negative feelings in the bud. Like I said, if I let things weigh heavily on my mind, I'd just be so distracted, and my staff would definitely notice if I was off my game. I have to set a good example for the office. So I just keep moving forward and focus on my responsibilities. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not the kind of answer you're looking for—am I misunderstanding what you mean by weighing heavily? I really want to be as helpful as I can, I just don't want to make it sound like something I'm agonizing over when I'm really not.
